does not hold anymore. The inconvenient of having chosen
A=+1
is that, unlike for
A=-1
, the class of a stated tangle depends on the framing (up to a sign) because of the skein relation

. This is the main reason why we will need to consider spin functions in the next subsection.

5.2.
Spin functions

Let us define a functor
\tau\in\widehat{\operatorname{BT}}_{\mathbb{Z}}=[\operatorname{BT}^{op},\operatorname{Mod}_{\mathbb{Z}}]
. For
n\geq 0
set
\tau(\mathbf{H}_{n}):=\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbf{H}_{n};\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})
and for
\mu:\mathrm{H}_{a}\to\mathrm{H}_{b}
set
\tau(\mu):=\mu^{*}:\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbf{H}_{b};\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbf{H}_{a};\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})
. Let us identify
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbf{H}_{1};\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})
with
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
.

Definition 5.4
.

For
\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{c}}^{(1)}
, a
spin function
is a natural morphism
w:P_{M}\to\tau
such that
w_{1}\left(\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[width]{BT_Theta.eps}}}}}}}$}\right)=1\in\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\cong\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbf{H}_{1};\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})
.

So a spin function is a collection of maps
w_{n}:P_{n}(\mathbf{M})\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbf{H}_{n};\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})
such that
w_{1}\left(\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[width]{BT_Theta.eps}}}}}}}$}\right)=1
and such that for every morphism
\mu:\mathbf{H}_{a}\to\mathbf{H}_{b}
, the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{b}(\mathbf{M})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{a}(M)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbf{H}_{b};\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbf{H}_{a};\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{*}}

Remark 5.5
.

(1)

A spin function is completely determined by the map
w_{1}:P_{1}(\mathbf{M})\to\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
. Indeed, let
[\beta_{i}]\in\mathrm{H}_{1}(\mathbf{H}_{n};\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})
be the class of a simple closed curve encircling the
i
-th hole of
\mathbb{D}_{n}\times\{1/2\}\subset\mathbf{H}_{n}
once, and consider the isomorphism
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\mathbf{H}_{n};\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})\cong(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})^{n}
sending
\varphi
to
(\varphi([\beta_{1}]),\ldots,\varphi([\beta_{n}]))
.
If
\alpha=\alpha^{(1)}\cup\ldots\cup\alpha^{(n)}
is a
n
-bottom tangle with connected components
\alpha^{(i)}
, by naturality of
w
and under the above isomorphism one has
w_{n}(\alpha)=(w_{1}(\alpha^{(1)}),\ldots,w_{1}(\alpha^{(n)}))
so the maps
w_{n}
are determined by
w_{1}
.

(2)

For
\alpha
a
1
-bottom tangle and
s
a state on
\alpha
, the class
(-1)^{w_{1}(\alpha)}[\alpha,s]\in\mathcal{S}_{+1}(\mathbf{M})
does not depend on the framing of
\alpha
thanks to the condition
w_{1}\left(\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[width]{BT_Theta.eps}}}}}}}$}\right)=1
and to the skein relation

.

(3)

When
\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{\Sigma}\times I
is a thickened marked surface, then the set of spin functions is in one-to-one correspondence with the set of isomorphism classes of spin structures on
\Sigma
(hence the name ”spin functions”). Indeed, fix a Riemannian metric on
\Sigma
and let
\pi:UM\to M
be the unitary tangent bundle. Let
[\theta]\in\mathrm{H}_{1}(UM;\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})
be the homology class of a loop in
UM
whose projection in
M
is the constant point
p_{M}
and such that
\theta
makes a full positive twist in the fiber
\pi^{-1}(p_{M})
. For
w
a spin function, one associates
\mathbf{w}:\mathrm{H}_{1}(UM;\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})\to\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
a group morphism such that
\mathbf{w}([\theta])=1
as follows. Every class
[\alpha]\in\mathrm{H}_{1}(UM;\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})
can be represented by a
1
-bottom tangle
\alpha
(where we isotope both endpoints to
p_{M}
) and we set
\mathbf{w}([\alpha]):=w_{1}(\alpha)
. That
\mathbf{w}
is a group morphism follows from the naturality with respect to the morphism
\mu:\mathbf{H}_{2}\to\mathbf{H}_{1}
of Figure
5
and that
\mathbf{w}([\theta])=1
follows from the fact that
w_{1}\left(\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[width]{BT_Theta.eps}}}}}}}$}\right)=1
. The morphism
\mathbf{w}
defines a (regular) double covering
p:\widetilde{U\Sigma}\to U\Sigma
and the condition
w([\theta])\neq 0
ensures that each fiber of
\pi
lifts to a non trivial double covering by
p
. Since
\operatorname{Spin}(2)
is the only non trivial double covering of
\operatorname{SO}(2)
, the composition
\pi\circ p:\widetilde{U\Sigma}\to\Sigma
is a principal
\operatorname{Spin}(2)
bundle over
U\Sigma
, i.e. a spin structure (see Milnor
[
Mil63
]
for details).

5.3.
Representation and character schemes

Let
\Gamma
be a finitely presented group and consider the commutative ring

\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\Gamma)]:={\raisebox{1.99997pt}{$\mathbb{Z}[X^{\gamma}_{ij},i,j\in\{+,-\},\gamma\in\Gamma]$}\left/\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$(M_{\alpha}M_{\beta}=M_{\alpha\beta},\det(M_{\alpha})=1,\alpha,\beta\in\Gamma)$}\right.},

where
M_{\gamma}=\begin{pmatrix}X^{\gamma}_{++}&X^{\gamma}_{+-}\\
X^{\gamma}_{-+}&X^{\gamma}_{--}\end{pmatrix}
is a
2\times 2
matrix with coefficients in the polynomial ring
\mathbb{Z}[X^{\gamma}_{ij}]
(so the relation
M_{\alpha}M_{\beta}=M_{\alpha\beta}
represents in fact four relations). Set
\mathcal{O}[\operatorname{SL}_{2}]:={\raisebox{1.99997pt}{$\mathbb{Z}[x_{ij},i,j\in\{-,+\}]$}\left/\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$(x_{++}x_{--}-x_{+-}x_{-+}-1)$}\right.}
.
The set of characters
\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Alg}}\left(\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\Gamma)],\mathbb{C}\right)
is in bijection with the set of representations
\rho:\Gamma\to\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
. The group
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{C})
acts (on the left) by conjugacy on the set of representations by the formula:

(g\cdot\rho)(\gamma):=g\rho(\gamma)g^{-1},\quad\mbox{ for all }g\in\operatorname{SL}_{2},\gamma\in\Gamma.

The above action is algebraic, i.e. induced by a comodule map
\Delta^{R}:\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\Gamma)]\to\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\Gamma)]\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{O}[\operatorname{SL}_{2}]
defined by the formula:

\Delta^{R}(X_{ij}^{\gamma}):=\sum_{a,b=\pm}X_{ab}^{\gamma}\otimes x_{ia}S(x_{bj}).

For instance

\Delta^{R}(X_{++}^{\gamma})=X_{++}^{\gamma}\otimes x_{++}x_{--}+X_{-+}^{\gamma}\otimes x_{+-}x_{--}-X_{+-}^{\gamma}\otimes x_{-+}x_{++}-X_{--}^{\gamma}\otimes x_{-+}x_{+-}.

Let
\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{M})]\subset\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\Gamma)]
be the subalgebra of coinvariant vectors.

Both
\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\Gamma)]
and
\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\Gamma)]
are finitely generated, however they might be non reduced, i.e. their nilradical
\sqrt{0}
might be non trivial.

Definition 5.6
.

The
\operatorname{SL}_{2}
-
representation scheme
is
\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\Gamma):=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\Gamma)])
. The
\operatorname{SL}_{2}
-
character scheme
is
\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\Gamma):=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\Gamma)])
.

The
\operatorname{SL}_{2}
-
representation variety
is
\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}^{red}(\Gamma):=\operatorname{Spec}({\raisebox{1.99997pt}{$\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\Gamma)])\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{C}$}\left/\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\sqrt{0}$}\right.})
. The
\operatorname{SL}_{2}
-
character variety
is
\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}^{red}(\Gamma):=\operatorname{Spec}({\raisebox{1.99997pt}{$\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\Gamma)])\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{C}$}\left/\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\sqrt{0}$}\right.})
.

Recall that to
\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\operatorname{c}}
one associates a canonical based point
p_{M}:=\iota_{M}(0)\in\mathbb{D}_{M}
. Since every compact
3
-manifold can be triangulated (see e.g.
[
Hem04
]
), we easily deduced that the fundamental group
\pi_{1}(\mathbf{M}):=\pi_{1}(M,p_{M})
is finitely presented.
Write
\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{M}):=\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\pi_{1}(\mathbf{M}))
and
\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{M}):=\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\pi_{1}(\mathbf{M}))
. In
[
KM17
, Corollary
1.3
]
, Kapovitch-Millson proved the existence of large families of
3
-manifolds for which
\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{M})
(and thus
\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{M})
) is non reduced, so it is important to distinguish between representation schemes and varieties. However, when
M
is a thickened surface,
\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{\Sigma}\times I)
is reduced (see
[
PS00
,
CM09
]
). An embedding
f:\mathbf{M}\to\mathbf{M}^{\prime}
between two marked
3
-manifolds induces a group morphism
f_{*}:\pi_{1}(\mathbf{M})\to\pi_{1}(\mathbf{M}^{\prime})
and thus a morphism
f_{*}:\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{M})]\to\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{M}^{\prime})]
of
\mathcal{O}[\operatorname{SL}_{2}]
comodules. We denote by
\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]:\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{c}}^{(1)}\to{\mathcal{O}[\operatorname{SL}_{2}]}-\operatorname{RComod}
the functor sending
\mathbf{M}
to
\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{M})]
.

Lemma 5.7
.

The following diagram is a left Kan extension:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\operatorname{c}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{BT}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}[\operatorname{SL}_{2}]}-\operatorname{RComod}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchoice{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=1.4875pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=1.4875pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=2.93999pt,depth=1.04124pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=2.1pt,depth=1.0082pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}}

In particular, one has:

\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{M})]\cong\mathchoice{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=1.4875pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=1.4251pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}\otimes_{\operatorname{BT}}\mathbb{Z}[P_{M}].

Proof.

For each
n\geq 0
, we fix a basis of the free group
\pi_{1}(\mathbf{H}_{n})
inducing an isomorphism
\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{H}_{n})]\cong\mathcal{O}[\operatorname{SL}_{2}]^{\overline{\otimes}n}
.
Let
L:=\operatorname{Lan}_{i}\mathchoice{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=1.4875pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=1.4251pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}
be the left Kan extension of
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=1.4875pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=1.4251pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}
along
i
given by the formula:

L(\mathbf{M}):=\mathchoice{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=1.4875pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=1.4251pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}\otimes_{\operatorname{BT}}\mathbb{Z}[P_{M}]=\int^{n\geq 0}\mathcal{O}[\operatorname{SL}_{2}]^{\overline{\otimes}n}\otimes\mathbb{Z}[P_{n}(\mathbf{M})].

By definition of the coend, one has

L(\mathbf{M})={\raisebox{1.99997pt}{$\left(\oplus_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{O}[\operatorname{SL}_{2}]^{\overline{\otimes}n}\otimes\mathbb{Z}[P_{n}(\mathbf{M})]\right)$}\left/\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\mathcal{J}$}\right.},

where
\mathcal{J}
is the ideal generated by elements
(\mu_{*}X\otimes\gamma)-(X\otimes\mu^{*}\gamma)
with
\mu:\mathbf{H}_{n}\to\mathbf{H}_{m}
,
\gamma\in P_{m}(\mathbf{M})
and
X\in\mathcal{O}[\operatorname{SL}_{2}]^{\overline{\otimes}n}
.

Let
\pi_{M}\in\widehat{\operatorname{BT}}
be the functor sending
\mathbf{H}_{n}
to
\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Gp}}(\pi_{1}(\mathbf{H}_{n}),\pi_{1}(\mathbf{M}))\cong\pi_{1}(\mathbf{M})^{n}
(the latter isomorphism is given by the fixed basis of
\pi_{1}(\mathbf{H}_{n})
). A
1
-bottom tangle
\alpha\subset M
defines an element
[\alpha]\in\pi_{1}(\mathbf{M})
by forgetting the framing and isotoping locally both endpoints of
\alpha
to
p_{M}
. For a
n
-bottom tangle
\alpha\in P_{n}(\mathbf{M})
with components
\alpha=\alpha^{(1)}\cup\ldots\cup\alpha^{(n)}
, ordered such that the heights of the endpoints
\partial\alpha^{(i)}
is bigger than the heights of the endpoints
\partial\alpha^{(i+1)}
, we set
[\alpha]:=([\alpha^{(1)}],\ldots,[\alpha^{(n)}])\in\pi_{1}(\mathbf{M})^{n}
.

Define a natural morphism
\eta^{M}:P_{M}\Rightarrow\pi_{M}
by sending
\alpha\in P_{n}(\mathbf{M})\cong P_{M}(\mathbf{H}_{n})
to
\eta^{M}_{n}(\alpha):=[\alpha]
. The map
\eta^{M}_{n}:P_{n}(\mathbf{M})\to\pi_{1}(\mathbf{M})^{n}
is clearly surjective and two bottom tangles
\alpha
,
\alpha^{\prime}
have the same image if and only if one can pass from
\alpha
to
\alpha^{\prime}
by a finite sequence of crossings changes
\hbox to17.47pt{\vbox to17.47pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 8.7359pt\lower-8.7359pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{ {}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\leftrightarrow\hbox to17.47pt{\vbox to17.47pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 8.7359pt\lower-8.7359pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{ {}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \color[rgb]{0.775,0.775,0.775}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -11.81 -11.81 M -11.81 -11.81 L -11.81 11.81 L 11.81 11.81 L 11.81 -11.81 Z M 11.81 11.81}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -7.87 10.43 L 7.87 -10.24}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -7.87 -10.24 L -1.97 -2.36}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.97 2.36 L 7.87 10.43}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
and twist moves

. Write
\alpha\sim\alpha^{\prime}
if
\alpha
and
\alpha^{\prime}
are related by such a finite sequence, so that
\eta^{M}_{n}
induces a bijection
{\raisebox{1.99997pt}{$P_{n}(\mathbf{M})$}\left/\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\sim$}\right.}\xrightarrow{\cong}\pi_{1}(\mathbf{M})^{n}
. We claim that

L(\mathbf{M}):=\mathchoice{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=1.4875pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=1.4251pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}\otimes_{\operatorname{BT}}\mathbb{Z}[P_{M}]\xrightarrow[id\otimes\eta^{M}]{\cong}\mathchoice{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=1.4875pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=1.4251pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}\otimes_{\operatorname{BT}}\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{M}],

i.e. that for
X\in\mathcal{O}[\operatorname{SL}_{2}]^{\overline{\otimes}n}
and
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in P_{n}(\mathbf{M})
which differ by some change of crossings
\hbox to17.47pt{\vbox to17.47pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 8.7359pt\lower-8.7359pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{ {}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \color[rgb]{0.775,0.775,0.775}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -11.81 -11.81 M -11.81 -11.81 L -11.81 11.81 L 11.81 11.81 L 11.81 -11.81 Z M 11.81 11.81}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -7.87 -10.24 L 7.87 10.43}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.87 -10.24 L 1.97 -2.36}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -1.97 2.36 L -7.87 10.43}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\leftrightarrow\hbox to17.47pt{\vbox to17.47pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 8.7359pt\lower-8.7359pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{ {}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \color[rgb]{0.775,0.775,0.775}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -11.81 -11.81 M -11.81 -11.81 L -11.81 11.81 L 11.81 11.81 L 11.81 -11.81 Z M 11.81 11.81}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -7.87 10.43 L 7.87 -10.24}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -7.87 -10.24 L -1.97 -2.36}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 1.97 2.36 L 7.87 10.43}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
, then
[X\otimes\alpha]=[X\otimes\alpha^{\prime}]\in L(\mathbf{M})
. Indeed, in such case, one has an endomorphism
\mu:\mathbf{H}_{n}\to\mathbf{H}_{n}
such that
\mu(\alpha)=\alpha^{\prime}
and such that
\eta^{M}(\mu)=id
. For such an endomorphism
\mu
whose action on
\pi_{1}(\mathbf{M})
is trivial, the induced morphism
\mu_{*}\in\operatorname{End}(\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{H}_{n})])
is the identity, therefore
\alpha\otimes X-\alpha^{\prime}\otimes X\in\mathcal{J}
, which proves the claim.

We can thus write

L(\mathbf{M})={\raisebox{1.99997pt}{$\left(\oplus_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{O}[\operatorname{SL}_{2}]^{\overline{\otimes}n}\otimes\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}(M)^{n}]\right)$}\left/\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\mathcal{J}^{\prime}$}\right.},

with
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
the image of
\mathcal{J}
though the quotient map.
On the other hand, write

\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{M})]={\raisebox{1.99997pt}{$A$}\left/\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$\mathcal{I}$}\right.},

where

A:={\raisebox{1.99997pt}{$\mathbb{Z}[X^{\gamma}_{ij},i,j\in\{+,-\},\gamma\in\pi_{1}(\mathbf{M})]$}\left/\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$(\det(M_{\alpha})=1,\alpha\in\pi_{1}(\mathbf{M}))$}\right.},

and
\mathcal{I}
is the ideal generated by the relations given by the matrix coefficients of the equalities
M_{\alpha}M_{\beta}=M_{\alpha\beta}
,
\alpha,\beta\in\pi_{1}(\mathbf{M})
. Let

\widetilde{\kappa}_{\mathbf{M}}:A\cong\oplus_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{O}[\operatorname{SL}_{2}]^{\overline{\otimes}n}\otimes\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{1}(M)^{n}]

be the isomorphism sending a monomial
X_{a_{1}b_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}\ldots X_{a_{n}b_{n}}^{\gamma_{n}}
to
[(x_{a_{1}b_{1}}\otimes\ldots\otimes x_{a_{n}b_{n}})\otimes(\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{n})]
. By definition of
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
, we have
\widetilde{\kappa}_{\mathbf{M}}(\mathcal{I})=\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
, so
\widetilde{\kappa}
induces an isomorphism

\kappa_{\mathbf{M}}:\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{M})]\cong L(\mathbf{M}).

That
\kappa_{\mathbf{M}}
is an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}[\operatorname{SL}_{2}]
comodules and that it is natural in
\mathbf{M}
are immediate consequences of the definitions. We thus have defined a natural isomorphism
\kappa:\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\cong L
. This concludes the proof.

∎

5.4.
Classical limit of stated skein modules

Let
\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{M}^{(1)}_{\operatorname{c}}
and
\gamma\in\pi_{1}(\mathbf{M})
. Let
T(\gamma)
be a tangle isotopic to
\gamma
with arbitrary framing with distinct endpoints
\partial{T}=\{s,t\}\in\mathbb{D}_{M}
such that
\gamma
is oriented from
s
(starting point) to
t
(target point). For
i,j\in\{+,-\}
, let
\gamma_{ij}:=[T(\gamma),s_{ij}]\in\mathcal{S}_{+1}(\mathbf{M})
be the class of the tangle
T(\gamma)
with state defined by
s_{ij}(s):=v_{i}
and
s_{ij}(t):=v_{j}
. The main result of this section is the following:

Theorem 5.8
.

Let
w
be a spin function.
We have a ring isomorphism
\eta_{w}:\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{M})]\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{S}_{+1}(\mathbf{M})
whose image on the generators
X_{ij}^{\gamma}
is given by

\eta_{w}\begin{pmatrix}X^{\gamma}_{++}&X^{\gamma}_{+-}\\
X^{\gamma}_{-+}&X^{\gamma}_{--}\end{pmatrix}:=(-1)^{w(\gamma)}\begin{pmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\gamma_{++}&\gamma_{+-}\\
\gamma_{-+}&\gamma_{--}\end{pmatrix}.

Moreover
\eta_{w}
is a morphism of
\mathcal{O}[\operatorname{SL}_{2}]
comodules.

In particular, we obtain a variant of a classical theorem of Bullock:

Corollary 5.9
.

One has an isomorphism
\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}({M})]\cong\mathcal{S}_{+1}(M)
sending a trace function
\tau_{\gamma}
to
(-1)^{w(\gamma)}[\gamma]
.

Remark 5.10
.

(1)

In the particular case where
\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{\Sigma}\times I
is a thickened marked surface, Theorem
5.8
was proved independently in
[
KQ19
]
and
[
CL19
]
using triangulations. An alternative proof using explicit finite presentations of stated skein algebras was also presented in
[
Kor20
]
.

(2)

Corollary
5.9
is closely related to a theorem of Bullock in
[
Bul97
]
who constructed an isomorphism between
\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{X}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{M})]
and the skein algebra
\mathcal{S}_{A=-1}(M)
evaluated in
A=-1
by sending a trace function
\tau_{\gamma}
to
-[\gamma]
. Putting these two results together, one obtains an isomorphism (depending on
w
) between the skein algebra in
A=-1
and the skein algebra in
A=+1
sending
[\gamma]
to
(-1)^{w(\gamma)+1}[\gamma]
. In the particular case where
\mathbf{M}=\mathbf{\Sigma}\times I
is a thickened surface (so
w
is given by a spin structure on
\Sigma
as explained in Remark
5.5
), the existence of such an isomorphism
\mathcal{S}_{+1}(\Sigma)\cong\mathcal{S}_{-1}(\Sigma)
was proved by Barett in
[
Bar99
]
.

Notations 5.11
.

\operatorname{BT}_{0}
and
\operatorname{BT}_{1}
of
\operatorname{BT}
. The objects of
\operatorname{BT}_{0}
and of
\operatorname{BT}_{1}
are the same than the objects of
\operatorname{BT}

\mathbf{H}_{n}
for

. Identify

and

.
The compositions in
\operatorname{BT}_{0}
and
\operatorname{BT}_{1}
are induced by the compositions in
\operatorname{BT}
after passing to the quotient. We thus have projection functors

\operatorname{BT}\xrightarrow{p^{\prime}}\operatorname{BT}_{1}\xrightarrow{p}\operatorname{BT}_{0}

which are the identity on objects. For
1\leq i\leq q
, let
\theta_{i}^{a}\in\operatorname{BT}_{1}(a,a)
be the bottom tangle

\theta_{i}^{a}:=\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\resizebox{}{}{{\hbox{{\includegraphics[width]{BT_theta_i.eps}}}}}}}$}.

Then
(\theta_{i}^{a})^{2}=id
and the
\theta_{i}^{a}
pairwise commute so they generate a group
G_{a}\cong(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})^{a}\subset\operatorname{BT}_{1}(a,a)
. This group naturally acts freely on the right of
\operatorname{BT}_{1}(a,b)
and the quotient map
p:\operatorname{BT}_{1}(a,b)\to\operatorname{BT}_{0}(a,b)
induces an isomorphism

\widetilde{p}:{\raisebox{1.99997pt}{$\operatorname{BT}_{1}(a,b)$}\left/\raisebox{-1.99997pt}{$G_{a}$}\right.}\xrightarrow{\cong}\operatorname{BT}_{0}(a,b).

Define also a section
s:\operatorname{BT}_{0}(a,b)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{BT}_{1}(a,b)
such that
s\circ p=id
by lifting a class
[\alpha]\in\operatorname{BT}_{0}(a,b)
to the class
[\alpha_{0}]\in\operatorname{BT}_{1}(a,b)
where the bottom tangle
\alpha_{0}
is chosen such that each of its component has self-linking number
0
. The section
s
defines an isomorphism
\operatorname{BT}_{1}(a,b)\cong\operatorname{BT}_{0}(a,b)\times G_{a}
. We will now denote a morphism
\mu\in\operatorname{BT}_{1}(a,b)
by
\mu=(\mu_{0},g)
where
\mu_{0}=s(\mu)
and
g\in G_{a}
is the unique element such that
\mu_{0}\cdot g=\mu
. Note that
s:\operatorname{BT}_{0}\to\operatorname{BT}_{1}
is a functor.

Note that the right
\operatorname{BT}
-module
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=1.4875pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=1.4251pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}
passes to the quotient to a right
\operatorname{BT}_{0}
module: this follows from the facts that for
\mu:\mathbf{H}_{a}\to\mathbf{H}_{b}
an embedding, the morphism
\mu_{*}:\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{H}_{a})]\to\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{H}_{b})]
only depends on the morphism
\mu_{*}:\pi_{1}(\mathbf{H}_{a})\to\pi_{1}(\mathbf{H}_{b})
i.e. only depends on the class of
\mu
in
\operatorname{BT}_{0}(a,b)
.
We defined during the proof of Lemma
5.7
an explicit isomorphism
\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{M})]\cong\mathchoice{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=1.4875pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=1.4251pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}\otimes_{\operatorname{BT}}\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{M}]
where
\pi_{M}
is a right
\operatorname{BT}_{0}
module obtained as a quotient of
P_{M}
. Therefore one has an isomorphism

\kappa_{\mathbf{M}}:\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{SL}_{2}}(\mathbf{M})]\cong\mathchoice{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}_{0}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=6.00002pt,depth=2.12502pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}_{0}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=4.20001pt,depth=2.125pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}_{0}}}{{\mathcal{O}[\mathcal{R}]\,\smash{\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=2.70009pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}_{0}}}\otimes_{\operatorname{BT}_{0}}\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{M}].

On the other hand, the right
\operatorname{BT}
-module
\mathchoice{{\mathcal{S}_{+1}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.46666pt,depth=1.98335pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{S}_{+1}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.46666pt,depth=1.98335pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{S}_{+1}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.82668pt,depth=1.55836pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{S}_{+1}\,\smash{\vrule height=2.71904pt,depth=2.07782pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}
passes to the quotient to a right
\operatorname{BT}_{1}
module: for
\mu:\mathbf{H}_{a}\to\mathbf{H}_{b}
an embedding, the fact that the morphism

is invariant under the skein relations

and

follows from the fact that the same skein relations hold in
\mathcal{S}_{+1}(\mathbf{M})
. By Lemma
5.1
, one has an isomorphism

\mathcal{S}_{+1}(\mathbf{M})\cong\mathchoice{{\mathcal{S}_{+1}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.46666pt,depth=1.98335pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{S}_{+1}\,\smash{\vrule height=5.46666pt,depth=1.98335pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{S}_{+1}\,\smash{\vrule height=3.82668pt,depth=1.55836pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}{{\mathcal{S}_{+1}\,\smash{\vrule height=2.71904pt,depth=2.07782pt}}_{\,\operatorname{BT}}}\otimes_{\operatorname{BT}}\mathbb{Z}[P_{M}]

which is explicited in the proof of Theorem
4.14
where it is denoted by
G^{-1}
. Let
\pi_{M}^{fr}
be the left
\operatorname{BT}_{1}
module sending
\mathbf{H}_{n}
to the quotient of
P_{n}(\mathbf{M})
by the relations
\hbox to17.47pt{\vbox to17.47pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 8.7359pt\lower-8.7359pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{ {}{{}}{}
